...which removes that [impurity] from the mixture is called a medicine not of the second order, but of the third and greater order. And because the excess of volatile substances—referred to as "earthiness"—is found to be removed through the methods of **calcination** <term>calcinatio|The alchemical process of heating a substance to high temperatures to drive off volatile matter and reduce it to a powder</term> and **reduction** <term>reductio|The process of restoring a calcined substance or oxide back to its metallic state</term>, it was therefore necessary to find a medicine of the second order. This medicine should mask the innate [impurities], soften what is hard, and harden what is soft; and in both hard and soft metals, it should achieve a completion that is not deceptive <note>original: "Sophiſticum", meaning a mere surface-level imitation or chemical trick</note> but perfectly establishes a true **Sun-making** <term>Solificum|The power to transform base metals into Gold (the Sun)</term> or **Moon-making** <term>Lunificum|The power to transform base metals into Silver (the Moon)</term> body.

## *On the administration of Medicines*
### *of the second order.*
### CHAPTER VIII.

Since it is clear, therefore, that in the soft bodies <note>In alchemy, "soft" metals typically refer to lead and tin, which have low melting points</note> alone, through the ingenious techniques of this craft, one cannot remove the rapid melting nor the impurity innate in the very root of their principles, it becomes necessary to seek a medicine. This medicine, when cast upon them <note>This refers to "projection," the final step where the elixir is added to the base metal</note>, should thicken their thinness, and by thickening them, harden them enough to withstand the heat of melting. In hard metals <note>Likely referring to iron or copper, which are harder to melt</note>, it should thin their density, and by thinning them, bring them to a sufficient speed of melting while retaining the properties of the fire. It should beautify the darkness <note>original: "fuſcedinem", referring to the dull or dark color of base metals</note> of both types of bodies by masking it, transforming the former into white [Silver] and the latter into a most perfect yellow <note>original: "citrinum", the color characteristic of gold</note>. However, this medicine does not differ from a medicine of the third order...

<meta>catchword: na (completing "medicina" on the following page)</meta>

<summary>This page defines "medicines of the second order" as agents that correct the physical properties of metals—such as hardness, softness, and color—to prepare them for transformation into gold or silver. It distinguishes these from third-order medicines, which are even more powerful and fundamental.</summary>

<header>FOURTH [BOOK]</header>

...[medicine] <note>completing the word "medicina" from the previous page</note>, except through the imperfection of a lesser preparation. It does not differ from itself—as is evident in its **projection** <term>proiectio|The final stage of the alchemical process, where the "philosopher's stone" or medicine is cast upon a molten base metal to transform it</term> upon various bodies and its acceptance of pigments—but rather it differs in the method of preparation. For the medicine for soft bodies <note>In the alchemical tradition, "soft" metals typically refer to lead and tin because of their low melting points</note> requires a different diligence of preparation, as it must thicken their "thinness" <note>referring to their lack of resistance to heat</note>; while the medicine for hard bodies <note>Such as iron or copper</note> must thin their density. The latter requires the method of consuming fire, while the former requires the management of preserving moisture.

## *On Supplying the Defects after the Administration of Medicines of the Second Order.*

### CHAPTER IX.

Let us therefore present a universal discourse regarding the medicines of this second order, complete with a certain and true determination. We shall first discuss all the **Lunar** <term>Lunares|Medicines or elixirs intended to create Silver, which is associated with the Moon</term> medicines for all bodies that fall short of perfection, along with the differences in their preparation. After that, we shall discuss the **Solar** <term>Solares|Medicines or elixirs intended to create Gold, which is associated with the Sun</term> medicines, likewise with their own specific differences. We have already proven in our discourses that **Sulphur** <term>Sulphur|In alchemy, one of the two primary principles of metals; it was often seen as the source of "combustion" or impurity that needed to be cleansed</term>, of whatever kind of perfection, is corruptive. **Mercury** <note>original: "Argentum vivum", literally "living silver"</note>, however, is the perfecting agent in the work of nature once the procedures <note>original: "regiminibus"</note> are completed. Therefore, we are imitators of nature in those things where it is not possible to follow her exactly through [natural] works. And likewise, Mercury, in the **Mastery** <term>Magisterium|The "Great Work" or the highest level of alchemical achievement</term> of this work...

The matter of this medicine is, by its nature, of a single kind, and it is that which is already sufficiently known <note>In this tradition, the "matter" usually refers to Mercury or a combination of Mercury and Sulfur</note>. Take it, then; and if you wish to work toward **Lunar** results according to the order previously sent to you, you must make yourself practiced. Prepare it, therefore, by the known methods of this **Mastery** <term>Magisterii|The Great Work or alchemical process</term>, the intent of which is that you divide the pure substance from it. One part you must **fix** <note>original: "figas" — Fixation is the process of making a volatile substance stable under heat so it does not evaporate</note>, and the other part set aside for **ceration** <note>original: "cerandum" — The process of softening a substance until it has a wax-like consistency and melts easily</note>. 

Following the whole Mastery in this way until you complete it, test its **fusion** <note>melting point</note>. If it melts suddenly, the work is perfected for "hard" bodies; if for "soft" bodies, it is the opposite <note>Hard bodies refer to metals like Iron or Copper; soft bodies refer to Lead or Tin. A medicine that melts instantly is better suited to penetrate the dense structure of hard metals</note>. For this medicine, when **projected** <note>The act of "Projection," or casting the medicine onto molten base metal</note> upon any one of the imperfect bodies, changes that body perfectly into the **Lunar** [Silver] body—provided that the known preparations precede this medicine. If [those preparations] are not present, it does not leave the metal diminished, but it only completes the perfection to the degree that depends on the administration of this order of medicine; for it completes only so much by projection alone without a preceding administration.

Furthermore, when projected upon **Silver** <term>Lunam|Literally "the Moon," the alchemical name for Silver</term>, it perfects it into a very special Solar complement <note>This describes the transmutation of silver into gold</note>.

A common alchemical trope: the fear that the "unworthy" or "wicked" might steal or misuse the secret knowledge.</note>, we relate the entire fulfillment of this **Mastery** <term>Mastery|The "Magisterium" or the complete alchemical process required to create the Stone</term> in a concise, complete, and well-known manner.

Its purpose is that, by means of **sublimation** <term>sublimation|The process of heating a substance into a vapor and letting it collect as a solid again to purify it</term>, the **stone** and its additive are most perfectly cleansed; and thereafter, by skillful methods, let the volatile part be fixed by them; then let the fixed part be made volatile, and again the volatile be made fixed. And do this so many times—making the fixed volatile and the volatile fixed—until it demonstrates an easy **fixation** <term>fixation|Making a substance stable in fire so it no longer evaporates</term> when subjected to fire.

In this order is completed the most precious **secret** <term>secret|original: "arcanum"; a hidden mystery or powerful chemical truth</term>, which is above every secret of the sciences of this world and is an incomparable treasure. Indeed, you must practice it with the greatest persistence of labor and with a long duration of immense meditation; for with these things you shall find it, and without them, you shall not. 

For this medicine, the repetition of good administration, with such careful industry, can result in the preparation of the stone until it transforms **quicksilver** <term>quicksilver|Mercury (Argentum vivum)</term> into an infinite amount of true **gold-maker** <note>original: "Solificum"; a substance that transforms other metals into Gold (Sol)</note> and **silver-maker** <note>original: "Lunificum"; a substance that transforms other metals into Silver (Luna)</note>. This depends on nothing except its multiplication or the frequent repetition of its preparation.
